Cover of Regular Meeting 
The subjects to be discussed or considered or upon which any formal action may be taken are as 
listed below. Items do not have to be taken in the order shown on this meeting notice. 
Unless removed from the consent agenda, items identified within the consent agenda will be 
acted on at one time. 
1. PLEDGE OF ALLEGIANCE  
2. RECOGNITION  
3. ADDITIONS & DELETIONS

6. PERSONNEL  
A. CONSIDERATION:  Elementary & Secondary  
* All meetings, except closed sessions, are open to the public.  The Board welcomes citizens’ comments.  The public participa tion 
portion of the meeting is limited to one -half hour for discussion of agenda items and one -half hour for non -agenda items.  Each 
person will be allowed to speak for up to three minutes, except where the number of speakers exceeds the time limit.  In thos e 
instances, the President may reduce the time limit for each speaker in order to accommodate all of the individuals who wish to 
address the Board of Education.  In any event, no one’s right to address the Board will be denied.

11. ADJOURNMENT  
* All meetings, except closed sessions, are open to the public.  The Board welcomes citizens’ comments.  The public participa tion 
portion of the meeting is limited to one -half hour for discussion of agenda items and one -half hour for non -agenda items.  Each 
person will be allowed to speak for up to three minutes, except where the number of speakers exceeds the time limit.  In thos e 
instances, the President may reduce the time limit for each speaker in order to accommodate all of the individuals who wish to 
address the Board of Education.  In any event, no one’s right to address the Board will be denied.

BOARD OF EDUCATION

BOARD MEETING MINUTES       JANUARY 15, 2013 
       SERVICES BUILDING           
  President  Philippart  called  the  meeting  to  order  at  7:13 
p.m. 
In  addition  to  Ms.  Philippart,  present  were  Board 
members Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, Miletti, Schmidt and 
Underwood.   Also  present  were  Superintendent  Fowler; 
administrators  Birmingham,  Jolly,  Keeling,  Lesser, 
Machesky,  Rajter,  and  Witt;  and  approximately  60 
members of the audience.  
RECOGNITION  
Senior Scholar Athletes

sports.  These students have maintained a 3.5 cumulative 
grade  point  average  while  participating  in  an  athletic 
program.  The students recognized were:

Board of  Education 
In  honor  of  National  School  Board  Recognition  month, 
the  Troy  School  District  recognized  a  very  important 
group  of  individuals  who  are  tireless,  professional,  and 
passionate  about  improving  the  opportunities  of  our 
students: The Board of Education.   Board members were 
recognized  with  a  gift  to  illustrate  the  impact  they  have

6. B.  PERSONNEL 
CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment –  
    Director of Technology 
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of Mr. Mark Washington to the 
position of Director of Technology .  Mr. Washington  comes to Troy  with a 
combined 18 years of experience as a Director of Technology with in the Port 
Huron Area School District , Wayland Union Schools and Marysville Schools. In 
total, he has 22 years experience working in the technology area for public 
schools. Mr. Washin gton received a Bachelor of Science degree in Technical 
Communications with a minor in Management from Western Michigan University 
and is working towards completi on of  his Masters degree in Management 
Information Systems from Central Michigan University.  
With your approval, Mr. Mark Washington  will begin his duties as Director of 
Technology effective February 11, 2013, or as soon as possible thereafter.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below: 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by         
 Supported by        
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Board of Education issues a two-year 
probationary administrative contract without tenure in the administrative position 
to Mr. Mark Washington, who will begin his duties as Director of Technology 
effective February 11, 2013, or as soon as possible thereafter.

between the MOST LIKELY and HIGH projections.

by two (2) plus the HIGH divided by three (3).

 7. B.   02.05.13 
7. CURRICULUM 
B. CONSIDERATION:   Schools Closed to Open Enrollment for  
       2013/2014 
Administration has reviewed the enrollment for elementary, middle, and high 
school attendance areas.  Our goal has been to maximize the enrollments at 
both Athens and Troy High School resulting in both schools being closed to 
open enrollment for the past several years. For the 2013/2014 school year, it 
is recommended that 25 spots be available for 9th grade students scheduled 
to go to Troy High School but wishing to attend Athens High School.  It is 
projected Troy High School will continue to maintain optimal enrollment and, 
due to some continued demographic shifts, it is appropriate to consider a 
limited num ber of open enrollments be made available for the 201 3/2014 
school year to Athens High School.  It is therefore recommended that Athens 
High and Troy High School be closed to open enrollment, with the exception 
of the 25  openings made available for incomin g ninth graders only, who live 
in the Troy High School attendance area but wish to attend Athens High 
School for the 201 3/2014 school year.  Due to limited space, the district will 
be accepting applications from February 6 – 22, 2013. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Troy Board of Education adopts a resolution to 
close Athens High and Troy High School to open enrollment for the 201 3/2014 school 
year, with the exception of not more than 25 ninth grade students who reside in the 
Troy High School attendance area , but wish to attend Athens High School.  Students 
with siblings attending Athens Hi gh School and currently enrolled in grades 9 – 11 will 
be allowed to open enroll to Athens for the 2013/2014 school year.  
Yes: __________ 
No: __________

8. BUSINESS 
A. CONSIDERATION:  Authorization for Preparation of an 
Application for Preliminary Qualification 
of Bonds 
  The District is seeking to include a School Improvement Bond issue 
on the ballot for the November 2013 election.  This resolution will 
authorize administration to proceed with the preparation of an 
Application for Preliminary Qualification of Bonds. 
________________________________________________________ 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the resolution below. 
 Moved by:       __________________________ 
 Supported by:     __________________________ 
 WHEREAS, the Board of Education is contemplating undertaking v arious 
capital improvement projects (the “Projects”) throughout the School District;  
 WHEREAS, the Board of Education is further contemplating the financing 
of the proposed Projects with the issuance of voter approved capital 
improvement bonds; 
 WHEREAS, the Administration has prepared an initial draft of a School 
Improvement Bond Proposition, attached hereto as Exhibit A, which sets forth a 
preliminary description of the Projects to be financed with the bonds; 
 WHEREAS, the Board is of the opinion that  if a bond proposition is 
successfully approved by the voters, the bonds issued should be qualified under 
the Michigan School Bond Qualification and Loan Program; and 
 WHEREAS, the Board is contemplating submitting the bond proposition 
to the electors at a future school election date and desires to authorize the 
Superintendent to request preliminary qualification for the proposed bonds from 
the Department of Treasury as required under the Michigan School Bond 
Qualification and Loan Program. 
 NOW, THEREFOR E, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE BOARD OF 
EDUCATION OF THE TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT, OAKLAND COUNTY, 
MICHIGAN, THAT: 
168
 8. A.           02.05.13 
 1. The Superintendent and/or her designee is authorized to select and 
work with the architect, bond counsel, construction manager, technology 
designer and financial advisor and take the necessary action required to prepare 
an Application for Preliminary Qualification of Bonds for the submission to the 
Department of Treasury and to attend a Preliminary Qualification meeting with 
the Department of Treasu ry to review the Application for Preliminary 
Qualification of Bonds.  
 2. A resolution approving the submission of a final draft of the 
Application for Preliminary Qualification of Bonds which will include the final Bond 
Proposition as approved by the Dep artment of Treasury will be presented to the 
Board of Education at a future meeting. 
 3. All resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they conflict with 
the provisions of this resolution are hereby rescinded.

years the proposed bonds may be outstanding, exclusive of refunding, is not 
more than fiftee n (15) years; and the estimated simple average annual millage 
that will be required to retire the proposed bonds over _______ (___) years is

 C. CONSIDERATION:  Web Filtering Solution 
  Due to ineffective Internet content filtering, challenging configuration 
procedures and inadequate user support, bids were sought for web 
filtering products.  These bids were  reviewed by Mr. Ken Crawford, 
Interim Director of Technology,  and Mr. Mark Rajter , Assistant 
Superintendent, Business Services, and it is recommended the bid be 
awarded as stated below. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the bid for web filter be awarded to Phantom 
Technologies in the amount of $ 44,768.00 and that this amount be approved as 
an expenditure of the 2012-2013 General Fund.   
Yes: ____________ 
No: ____________ 
192
Memorandum 
 TO: Dr. Barbara Fowler 
  Superintendent 
Technology 
 FROM: Ken Crawford 
 Center 
 RE: Purchase of Web Filter  
4420 Livernois 
Troy, MI 48098 
Due to ineffective Internet content filtering, challenging configuration procedures, and inadequate 
user support, Merri Lynn Colligan began research in the Fall of 2012 to replace the current web 
filtering product with one that would meet the needs of Troy Schools students and staff.  In addition to 
the filtering and configuration difficulties, the current web filter, Deep Nines, does not provide granular 
settings for groups of users or effective reporting of user activity. 
Merri Lynn arranged for trial testing of two web filtering products, one manufactured by Barracuda, 
the other manufactured by Phantom Technologies.  Each of these products were tested for a period 
of 30 days on the Troy Schools network.  The goal of the trial was to identify a product that would 
meet the needs of Troy students and staff, while addressing the shortcomings of the current Deep 
Nines web filter. 
At the conclusion of the testing period, I sought bids for the Barracuda and Phantom Technologies 
web filters.  Two bids were available for the Barracuda product and three bids were available for the 
Phantom Technologies product.  One of the Phantom Technologies bids is offered via an Oakland 
Schools cooperative purchasing agreement that represents a significantly discounted price. 
I analyzed the trial testing results and bid responses to determine the web filtering product that would 
best meet the needs of Troy Schools.  The Phantom Technologies iBoss 14500 web filter costs 
$6,964 more than the Barracuda 810 spread over the three-year subscription period, but the iBoss 
14500 web filter offers significant advantages over the Barracuda 810.  The advantages of the iBoss 
14500 include: 
 Redundant web filter in place, no failover to open Internet access 
 Redundant power supplies 
 Better reporting system to track issues with users 
 More granular control of YouTube EDU with teacher video libraries 
 Recommended by Oakland Schools and Troy Schools technology staff 
Recommendation: 
Based upon my analysis, The Technology Department recommends the purchase of the iBoss 14500 
web filter from Phantom Technologies in the amount of $44,768. The award includes a three (3) year 
enterprise subscription for 5,000 nodes. The funding for this purchase will be the General Fund 
budget.

196
ADVERTISING & DISTRIBUTION OF MATERIALS IN THE SCHOOLS 
K‐1300  
K‐1303  COMMERCIAL ADVERTISING 
  No  commercial  material  which  causes  substantial  and  material 
interference  and/or  disruption  with  normal  school  activities  may  be 
distributed,  posted,  or  displayed  on  or  within  any  district‐owned 
property  without  the  written  consent  of  superintendent  or  designee.  
The  display  of  material  on  personal  vehicles  while  parked  on  school 
property  during  school  hours  is  permitted,  provided  it  does  not  cause 
substantial  or  material  interference  and/or  disruption  with  normal 
school activities. 
Further,  absent  the  express  written  consent  of  the  superintendent  or 
his/her  designee,  all  school  personnel,  including  contracted  personnel 
assigned  to  the  school  district,  shall  be  restricted  from  utilizing  Troy 
School  District  resources,  technology,  including  District  e‐mail,  and 
student/parent contact information received while engaged in duties for 
the Troy School District for commercial purposes.   
Adopted:  May 3, 1976 
Revised:  January 16, 1979 
  April 21, 1998 
  April 20, 2010
  June 19, 2012
